# P0 Spike — Task List

**Gate (charter §Milestones):** full index of 20,000 notes < 30 s · save→index < 50 ms p95 · cold start < 1 s (window visible < 400 ms).
**Requirements in scope (7, the whole P0 load):** FR-VLT-02, NFR-PRF-01, NFR-PRF-03, NFR-PRF-04, NFR-PRF-08, NFR-REL-02, NFR-MNT-02.
**Stories:** US-35 (primary), US-01, US-07. **ADRs:** 0001, 0002, 0003, 0006, 0007.
**Also settled by P0:** assumption A-06, architecture §1.2, open design question 1 (parse pool vs index writer), RISK-002.

Each teammate works in a git worktree under `.worktrees/<role>` and branches per task as `task/<id>-<slug>` from `main`; the lead merges after code-reviewer approval. See `docs/04-implementation/coding-standards.md`. Wave 1 (D1, B1, I1) was already in flight when the repository was created and lands directly on `main`. Status is maintained by the lead.

> ## 🔴 BUILDS: **FROZEN** — #95 is taking NFR-PRF-03, the last unmeasured gate number
>
> **Declared by the lead. No teammate runs a cargo build, a cargo test, or anything that writes to a `target/` until this banner turns green.** F-327 records 35% session-to-session movement on unchanged code and F-367's confirmed second cause is two cargo processes sharing one `target/`. **This is a scheduling constraint, not advice.** If you are idle, stay idle; if you are mid-build, finish and stop.
>
> **The tree #95 measures is `c60dfdd`** — `main` with **#119 merged** (`690b70c`) and **#94 merged**. **Scoped precisely, because the first version of this line over-credited the reviewer and they corrected it:** code-reviewer verified **`690b70c`** (all code files byte-identical to the approved `8460e32`) and verified that **#94 *would* merge cleanly** (merge-base `54a58e6`, no file touched by both sides). They have **not** mutated `c60dfdd` itself, and the eight #94 mutations ran against `d30aa34`, whose binary predates #119. **Two clean-merge facts are good evidence and are not the same as having mutated the composed tree** — that attestation comes after the freeze lifts. F-429 is satisfied on the merges and *inferred*, not demonstrated, on the composition.
>
> **How to read a zero, and this changes since F-450 was written.** #119 moved the terminal publish to *after* the slot fills, which can only ever **reduce** `handle_missing`, never inflate it — so it cannot flatter the p95 and is no reason to hold the run. But F-450's *"if the count goes to zero, that is #119's answer arriving from the other direction"* was written while #119 was unmerged. **On this tree a zero no longer distinguishes *the pre-warm worked* from *#119 closed the window* from *both*, and must not be recorded as having measured F-382's window at all** — #119's own 35.4 µs / 191 µs (F-462) stays the measurement of record. **A non-zero is now more interesting, not less:** a residual surviving both the pre-warm and the ordering fix is a third cause nobody has named, and it must not be filed under F-382 without a fresh diagnosis.
>
> **test-engineer: take #95.** 1,000 saves, `--profile reference`, canonical 20k vault, pre-warmed. Report `handle_missing` at zero or non-zero — **a residual is F-382's window measured a second way and it goes to #119's record, never absorbed.**
>
> **The clearance on F-435 and the pre-warm survivor is ATTESTED (F-475, F-476).** The reviewer had in fact sent both sentences immediately before the declaration and they queued behind it — **so the record was right about the sequence and wrong about the attestation**, and this line is the correction. code-reviewer re-derived both by mutation rather than quoting their own earlier claim, and both confirm the lead's derivation. F-435 closes on `degraded` gating `passed()` — *fail the number, not the run* — and the survivor is a SHOULD-FIX **only while `handle_missing` stays a hard zero; soften that and it becomes blocking the same day.** Three asks did not produce the two sentences, so: #95 runs 1,000 saves, and a short series cannot post a gate number either way — `indicative` travels on `Outcome` (F-448) and `degraded` gates `passed()` (mutation-verified). A failed pre-warm drives `handle_missing` non-zero and the run is refused outright. **If either derivation is wrong, the freeze shuts and the number is void.**
>

## Resume task list (from #90)

Two rulings by the owner, taken 2026-09-08, set this wave's shape.

1. **S2 is in the P0 gate.** The charter's P0 deliverable names "CodeMirror 6 mounted"; `app/` holds only generated bindings and `crates/serat-app/webview/` is a static shell. A cold-start figure taken against a shell with no editor framework in it is the flattering measurement, and this spike's own recorded lesson is about those.
2. **NFR-PRF-01 is re-baselined with the webview term rather than opening C-07.** The diagnosis on record is that the requirement was written without a webview-startup term in it, not that the implementation is slow: Serat's own main-thread work is 4.7-5.6 ms against 596-865 ms inside `WebviewWindowBuilder::build`. **The re-baseline is therefore a split, not a slackening** - a budget on the platform term and a budget on Serat's term - and it lands *after* the frontend is mounted and cold start re-measured, so the new number is not set against the shell S2 replaces. It is bounded to one platform (F-96: WebView2 is one of three webviews).

**Where the four gate numbers stand — corrected 2026-09-09, and the previous version of this paragraph was contradicted by the table directly beneath it (F-454's class, in the lead's own file).** Full rebuild: **PASS, gate-quality** — 27,693.5 ms against the 30,000 ms limit, 3.2% spread, `concurrent_build_processes: 0`, ruled by #96. *(This paragraph had said "passes on the best readings but not gate-quality" at 19.0 s against 24.4/27.1/36.4/37.6 s; that was true before #96 identified the spread as contention and attested a clean window, and #96's row has said so ever since.)* Cold start: **measured and missed**, `window_visible` p95 **1575.6 ms** against 400 and `vault_usable` p95 **1577.2 ms** against 1000, from #100 with the real frontend mounted — Serat term 201/234 ms, platform term 1117/1687 ms, warm-vault and scanner-disabled on both counts. *(The 1094/1088 ms figures this paragraph carried were pre-CodeMirror, taken against the static shell S2 replaced — the flattering measurement the resume ruling was written to prevent, left standing here for a day after #100 superseded it.)* Save-to-index: **still the only unmeasured one**, and now unblocked — #94's instrument recovered, #119 merging ahead of it, #95 behind the freeze. NFR-PRF-08: a Must that **cannot be satisfied at all** while CI produces no jobs (F-384), which is infrastructure and not a code gap. The 50k run is not a charter clause. **Test count, attributed rather than guessed:** **514 passed / 0 failed across 43 test binaries**, run by test-engineer on `task/94-prf03-real-save-path` @ `729d9b6` (main plus #94), with `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` and `fmt --check` clean. The lead did not re-run it — a second `cargo test --workspace` would contend for a `target/` another role is building in (F-367). The previous figure here was 455/0 across 40 binaries at `3f44a80`.

| 120 | `[P0/impl] Vault identity is a path string, so an equivalent path forces a full rebuild` | FR-VLT-02, NFR-REL-02, NFR-PRF-04 · C-02 · F-458, F-459 · ADR-0002 | rust-core-dev **with architect on the identity question** | `crates/serat-index` (`open_or_rebuild`, `index_meta.vault_root`) | — | **design ruled, implementation pending rust-core-dev.** Architect: canonicalise on record *and* on comparison — which `schema/001_init.sql:120` and §3.4 step 5 already specified, so the code is catching up with the design rather than changing it — **and demote the mismatch from a rebuild trigger to a reported signal.** The binding half is the second: canonicalisation shrinks the false-mismatch set and cannot empty it (a remapped drive letter or reconnected share re-canonicalises with no user action), and each false mismatch is a silent 28.6 s rebuild that NFR-REL-02's log cannot tell from real corruption. Record the new root, report it, fall through to reconciliation. *"Record something that is not a path"* rejected with reasons, not deferred. **Two obligations on the implementer:** confirm `asset_path`/`sidecar_path` are vault-relative (`001_init.sql:154-155` documents `notes.path` that way and says nothing about these two, and half one depends on it); and carry **ADR-0002's text in the same commit** — no new ADR, but the trigger it documents is changing and F-443's class forbids leaving the document behind. **rust-core-dev's objection is absent, not waived** (no reachable session at ruling time): the expected pushback is that if reconciliation-on-open assumes the index broadly matches the tree, discovering 20,000 differences through it may be worse than a rebuild rather than equal — in which case keep the rebuild but trigger it on reconciliation's own finding rather than on a string comparison. |

### Lead rulings, 2026-09-09 — F-450, and what #119 is now

**F-450 — a benchmark that waits for "usable" and then saves is measuring a vault mid-rebuild, and "usable" never promised otherwise.** F-449 recorded the 1000/1000 as evidence that `IndexActor::spawn` fills the `IndexHandleSlot` after publishing the status that unblocks `wait_for_usable`. That ordering **is** in the code and F-382 named it first, but it is not what produced this failure. `Startup::mark_index_settled` settles on the actor's first non-`Opening` state *by design*, and the reason is written above its own signature: the shell must come up on the vault that needs a rebuild most, so it waits for the state to be **known**, not **good**. Against a cold canonical 20k vault, `--exit-after-ready` returns seconds before any handle exists, and a thousand saves with no delay between them all land inside that window. **A near-certain miss over a multi-second rebuild is not the same defect as a race over a few instructions**, and attributing the first to the second inflates the second by three orders of magnitude.

This is the third time this wave that a measurement's failure was read as a product defect on the strength of the number alone. It is also the third time the diagnosis that would have corrected it existed only as an uncommitted diff. **The finder did the harder half — they read the code, found `mark_index_settled`'s own doc, and wrote the fix — and none of it was in the register**, so the lead numbered a product issue against a cause that had already been ruled out inside the team. `Commit before your session ends` is not housekeeping; it is the difference between a finding and a rumour.

**#119 is re-scoped, not closed, and tauri-app-dev owns deciding which.** The `spawn()` window is real code and F-382 called it narrow without measuring it. Two acceptable outcomes, and *stating which one you chose and why is the deliverable*:

- **Bound it.** Show the window is what F-382 says — instructions, not milliseconds — and record that with the evidence. An open caveat with a number beats a closed one without.
- **Close it cheaply.** If publishing the terminal state after `into_handle()` fills the slot costs nothing that `mark_index_settled`'s contract needs, do that. **The constraint is binding: the settled mark must still fire on `Rebuilding` and `Unavailable`.** A fix that makes the shell wait for `Ready` breaks §3.4's first precondition and is worse than the race.

**What it must not become** is a caller-side poll-and-back-off. That leaves the untruthful signal in place and pays for it at every call site forever.

**#94's `prewarm_index` is accepted in principle and carries one condition, because it is a change that makes a failing measurement pass.** NFR-PRF-03's words are *a note saved through Serat's own write path* in an open vault; racing a vault's first index build is not that requirement, and pre-warming is the honest setup, not a slackening. But this project's four worst defects were all instruments that flattered the figure, so: **the run must report `handle_missing` and any residual must be reported, never absorbed.** If the count goes to zero, that is #119's answer arriving from the other direction. If a residual survives the pre-warm, **that residual is F-382's window measured at last**, and it goes straight into #119. The conservation rule and the `degraded` field already force this; the condition is that nobody removes them to get a number.

**Order, and the freeze.** #94 recovered and committed → code-reviewer mutates the instrument at the branch tip → lead declares the build freeze → #95 measured → #101's NFR-PRF-03 blank filled. Unchanged from the standing ruling: **a number taken with an unmutated instrument is not a gate number here.**

### Lead rulings, 2026-09-08 (this file is the authority; act on it without further ack)

**#92 — discard the line, do not land it, and do not wait for tauri-app-dev.** The uncommitted one-liner in `.worktrees/rust-core` is `if !path.is_markdown()` → `if false`, which disables the **T-06 / FR-BIN-19** binary-write guard outright. It is **an abandoned mutation probe**, not unfinished work — a previous session's own testing, left behind when that session ended before reverting. Main's guard at `566149d` is correct and **T-06 was never exposed on any landed commit**. rust-core-dev: discard, rebase onto main, proceed. tauri-app-dev's agreement is no longer a precondition; the lead has taken the decision and informed them.

**The standing rule needs its other half, and this is it.** This wave opened by telling every teammate to recover the uncommitted work in their worktree, aimed at the three dirty worktrees found at resume. Applied blindly, that instruction would have committed a deliberately disabled security guard onto a branch under a message describing it as recovered work. **An uncommitted diff does not announce which kind of change it is**: some of it is unfinished work whose value is in being kept, and some is a probe whose entire value was in being reverted. The two are indistinguishable from the diff alone. So when recovering a dirty worktree, ask of every hunk whether it is a *deliberate weakening* — a guard turned off, a condition inverted, an early return inserted, a constant set so a test passes trivially. Those read as ordinary lines and are invisible once committed. rust-core-dev is recording this as a finding under its own F-number.

**#97 — rust-core-dev leads the design, test-engineer collaborates.** The existing `a_rebuild_over_an_existing_index_replaces_it_rather_than_merging` is not T1 and does not become it. Two gaps, both binding:

- **Counts are not equivalence.** Two indexes can agree on how many notes, blocks, fields and links they hold and disagree on every row. T1 asserts the *results* are identical — the rows a query returns, not the number of them. State in the test what the comparison does and does not cover.
- **Same-process is not the recovery path.** NFR-REL-02's criterion is a user restarting the application: delete or truncate the index, start, detect, rebuild, recover the same vault. A rebuild-twice in one process never exercises detection, never re-opens, and never reaches `open_or_rebuild`. **F-136 is the open caveat here** — I6's tests run against a fake vault, so classification and ordering are proven and real-disk behaviour is not. **T1 is where that closes**, so it runs against a real vault on real disk.

Make it fail for the right reason before making it pass — delete a row or corrupt one field after the rebuild and confirm it goes red. Six recorded instances in this project of an assertion that was correct while the setup never reached the path.

**Owner fields name roles, never instances (F-410).** A teammate read a ruling that named its own instance in the third person, concluded the work belonged to a different live session, and stood down from its own assignment. **An agent cannot reliably learn its own instance name** — the spawn prompt gives the role, the register gave the instance, and nothing connected them. So every owner field in the table above is a **role**: if you are the spawned `tauri-app-dev`, every row owned by `tauri-app-dev` is yours, and the same for every other role. Instance numbers appear only where the text is describing a session that is *not* the reader.

**Worktree ownership, superseding the second-set ruling: whoever is currently spawned as tauri-app-dev owns `.worktrees/tauri-app` and its HEAD outright — if you are reading this as the tauri-app-dev teammate, that is you.** (Phrased in the second person after F-410: the previous wording named the instance in the third person and its own owner read it as granting the worktree to somebody else.) The read-only ruling bound the *original* `tauri-app-dev` session, which shared that worktree and its git HEAD with `tauri-app-dev-2` while both were live. **Both of those sessions are stopped.** There is no concurrent user, so nothing is at risk: check out, rebase, commit and push freely there. **Asking before moving HEAD was exactly right** — the ruling did read as binding, the reflog showing this session's own earlier commits was ambiguous evidence, and the cost of being wrong was another session's lost work.

**#111 is a rebase, not a patch (F-409).** Its branch is 55 commits behind and its diff against main deletes all of `app/`. Rebase it onto current main, or reimplement `run_save_benchmark` there if the rebase is worse than the rewrite — **that judgement is the implementer's, not the lead's**, and it is worth saying out loud that my brief called this "a small fix", which it is not. F-402's bucketing fix lands after the rebase and is asserted the way code-reviewer specified: drive a save against a one-deep queue via `IndexHandle::with_queue_depth` and assert the timing lands in `writer_refused_ms` **and is absent from** `submitted_ms`.

**#113 is confirmed and cleared to commit on a fresh branch off current main** — main already has `Option<f64>`, `raf1_ms`/`raf2_ms` and `validate_paint_evidence()`; it lacks `AppState::mark_first_paint`. Land the port and the `mark_first_paint_forwards_the_real_argument_not_a_hardcoded_one` test; it closes F-397, which is live on main and was live when #100 was measured.

**#115 exists because a request that lives only in a message is a request half the team does not have.** test-engineer asked tauri-app-dev directly for `raf2_ms` to be stored on `Marks`/`Trace` — the same audit-only treatment `paint_evidence_ms` already gets — so #106's `frame_to_paint_gap_ms()` diagnostic reports a value instead of `None`. **They were right not to cross into another crate uninvited, and right to build the consuming side without waiting**, since every new field degrades to `None` on a trace that lacks it. The ask is small and correct and it had no row, so it was one unlanded message away from being lost. It closes F-403's remaining half: `raf1_ms`/`raf2_ms` now reach a Rust *parameter*, and this makes them reach the *trace*.

**Unblocked work for test-engineer while #111 and F-411 are held: code-reviewer's three SHOULD-FIXes on `task/91-93-97-test-recovery`.** None changes a number, all are yours, and one is a real defect of the class this wave keeps recording:

- **`a_guard_taken_before_a_pre_existing_dot_serat_is_created_still_protects_it` asserts the opposite of what its own comment claims.** Measured with a probe: a `.serat/` created by another party *after* `watch()` **is** deleted at drop, while the comment says the guard *"must still answer 'I did not create this'"*. No mutation kills this test that tests 1 and 3 do not already kill, so it adds no coverage and it misdescribes the behaviour — **F-107's shape, the text meaning something the code does not.** Either make the code match the comment or make the comment match the code, and say which you chose.
- **`cargo fmt --check` is red on the new bin** (two hunks).
- The branch touches `trace.rs` (+12, additive `paint_evidence_ms`), which an earlier ruling scoped to #106. **Same owner role and clearly needed — flagged rather than objected to, and it stands.**

**What is genuinely blocked and should stay blocked:** #94 behind #111's rebase, #95 behind #94, #107 behind #104 and #106, #110 behind the CI outage (F-384, with the owner). Do not start those.

**Merge condition on `task/406-frame-precedes-paint-diagnostic`, and it is two conditions, not one.** **(1)** The diagnostic must be **pinned** before it merges: deleting the check currently leaves 97/97 green, so an unpinned conversion trades an inert rejection for an unobserved diagnostic, which is not clearly better than what it replaces (F-439). The fix is `Option<String>` plus two assertions, with the deletion mutation run and watched red. **(2)** §3.4 now asserts, correctly and in the present tense, that `main` **rejects** on the frame-precedes-paint condition rather than reporting it — true today at `architecture.md:544`, and **false the moment that branch merges.** So the branch carries the obligation to move that tense in the same commit, or the design document acquires an F-368 at the instant of merge. §3.4 was merged first deliberately, on code-reviewer's recommendation, precisely so the sequence is the safe one.

**Ruling: NFR-PRF-03's figure is not quoted until code-reviewer has mutated the instrument that produced it.** #94 builds the instrument and #95 takes the number, and **the instrument has already been wrong twice in ways that flattered it** — F-402 (the bucketing could be bypassed with 94/94 green) and F-412 (the counter the bucketing reads could lie with 96/96 green). Both were found by mutation and neither by reading. code-reviewer asked for this themselves before being asked, and the reason is exact: *"I would want to mutate that one before any figure is quoted from it, for the same reason F-402 existed."*

**So the order is: #94 built → code-reviewer mutates → #95 measured → #101 quotes it.** Not #95 then review. **A number taken with an unmutated instrument is not a gate number here**, and this project has the receipts: three of the four defects that would have corrupted the original gate result (F-19, F-25, F-26, F-27) were in the measurement apparatus rather than in the product, every one flattering the figure. **The same rule applies to any re-run of NFR-PRF-01 or NFR-PRF-04 if their instruments change.**

**#101 starts now, and its dependency row understates what is available.** Two of the four numbers are **final and will not change**: NFR-PRF-04 **PASS** at 27,693.5 ms against 30,000 (3.2% spread, attested), and NFR-PRF-01 **FAIL** at `window_visible` p95 1575.6 ms against 400 and `vault_usable` p95 1577.2 ms against 1000 (INDETERMINATE on straddle). Draft against those and leave NFR-PRF-03 and NFR-PRF-08 **explicitly blank with what would fill them** — the same method that made #96's window produce a verdict instead of a debate. **Do not estimate NFR-PRF-03.**

Five things the report must carry that a reader will not reconstruct:

- **The split is the result, not a footnote.** NFR-PRF-01 fails while the Serat term (201/234 ms) would pass either legacy scalar budget on its own, and the unbudgeted platform term (1117/1687 ms) is what drags the aggregate over. Report the terms **before** the verdict, or the verdict reads as *"the implementation is slow"*, which the measurement says it is not.
- **F-405 bounds every cold-start figure including the one taken.** ~19% batch-to-batch on an attested-quiet machine. **#96 must not be generalised into "attestation makes figures stable"** — it showed NFR-PRF-04's drift was contention and gave 3.2%; that explanation does not transfer, and the cause here is unidentified.
- **NFR-PRF-08 is a Must that cannot be satisfied at all** while CI produces no jobs (F-384). A requirement unmet for reasons unrelated to the code must be *stated*, not omitted.
- **The mark was unguarded when NFR-PRF-01 was measured** (F-397) and **the conjunction is enforced nowhere** (F-403).
- **Carried, not adopted:** F-14, F-33 and F-34 are open from before this wave and outside P0 scope. List them, so the report does not read as though P0 left nothing open.

Two lessons from this wave belong in it. **Nine guards-that-cannot-fail are on record and the last three were each found inside the fix for the previous one** — F-392 inside F-377's fix, F-397 inside #105, F-402 inside #111 — which is one repeatable blind spot rather than nine unrelated slips. And **a green unit suite is not evidence a mechanism works**: 27 tests stayed green through two rounds of the paint guard being broken against the real application, and only a real WebView2 run caught either.

**#112's ownership — I ruled it one way, was wrong, and the normal split stands.** I gave vue-frontend-dev both halves as a bounded exception, on the reasoning that tauri-app-dev-2 was not replying and the handover was costing more than it saved. **tauri-app-dev-2 was replying; my message announcing the exception did not reach them, and they picked #112 up as the owner of `crates/serat-app` — correctly.** Both then wrote the Rust half in parallel: `task/112-null-paint-evidence-contract` (`126e10c`) and `task/112-paint-evidence-contract` (`3346c24`). **Second duplication of one task this wave from the same cause, and the same cause as the ID collisions: a ruling that exists only in an inbox is a ruling half the team does not have.** So: **tauri-app-dev-2 owns the Rust half, vue-frontend-dev owns `app/src/startup.ts`** — the ownership table's normal split, which was right before I overrode it. tauri-app-dev-2's `Option<f64>` with an explicit `PaintEvidenceRejected::absent()` is the version to keep, because it makes the absent case a **typed rejection raised by our code** rather than a failure at Tauri's IPC layer before our code runs. vue-frontend-dev's frontend commit stands. **The `raf1Ms`/`raf2Ms` conjunction check is architect's ruling**, correctly routed there rather than decided unilaterally, since it revisits F-388's territory.

**Merge order for the paint-mark stack, and it is blocked on F-395.** #98, #105 and #108 all touch the same guard and cannot merge until the duplication is resolved: **#105's `webview/boot.js` half is deleted in favour of #108's `app/src/startup.ts` gate**, since the former is build output and the latter is source. #105's Rust half stays exactly as it is. Then the order is **#98 → #108 → #105**, or all three together. **#100 cannot run until this lands**, because it measures the mounted frontend, so this is the critical path to the only cold-start number the wave can still produce.

**#98 and #108 merge together or neither, and the reason is not stylistic.** code-reviewer verified by mutation that **#98 alone leaves F-377's gap live on main**: `stubDoubleRaf` in `app/src/startup.test.ts` swaps rAF for `queueMicrotask` and counts nothing, and the only paint assertion is *"not called synchronously"* — which a single rAF satisfies. Collapsing the double rAF to a single one at `b2f6969` leaves **12 passed, 0 failed**. **F-377 reproduced inside the code replacing the file it was found in.** At `19fb1d0` the same mutation fails — `expected 10 to be 20`, 1 failed of 15 — because #108's distinct-increasing-timestamp stub pins the *nesting* rather than the wording, and `PaintEvidence` carries the callbacks' own `DOMHighResTimeStamp` plus a navigation anchor they cannot fake. **Splitting the stack would land the defect and leave the guard behind.**

**One line must move before #100, not after.** `App.vue`'s `onMounted` awaits `onIndexStatus(...)` — an IPC round trip — *before* calling `schedulePaintMark`, while `startup.ts`'s own doc says to call it as early as possible, so the rAF pair can miss the frame that actually painted first. The direction is conservative (it inflates, never flatters) which is why it is not blocking — **but what it inflates is `S_shell`, ADR-0014's binding Serat term, on the very number #100 measures and #104 baselines.** A round trip unrelated to painting has no business inside that interval. Cheaper to move now than to explain in the gate report.

**Ruling on the shared worktree: the original `tauri-app-dev` session goes read-only in `.worktrees/tauri-app`, permanently.** The hazard is worse than concurrent file edits — the two sessions share **one git HEAD**, so when `tauri-app-dev-2` checked out `task/105-paint-mark-evidence-guard`, the original session's checkout moved with it. **No `checkout`, `rebase`, `push`, `commit` or anything else that moves HEAD from the original session; `status`, `fetch` and `log` are fine.** Taking turns is not the intended model and I am not going to pretend it is: it is the failure the team already has a finding for, and the only reason nothing was lost is that the original session stopped and asked. `tauri-app-dev-2` owns that worktree and its HEAD. Nothing of the original's needs pushing — #90 landed on main by cherry-pick.

**Open question owed by tauri-app-dev, and it is the last thing keeping ADR-0014 out of `accepted`: the basis of §3.4's phase-table figures — medians or p95, one session or composed across several.** §Decision 6 requires the basis to travel with every figure the ADR quotes, and #99 restates those numbers, so until this is answered the document is non-compliant with its own rule. This is F-138's class ("report ranges, not points") sharpened by F-327: the same binary on the same vault has read 19.0, 24.4, 27.1, 36.4 and 37.6 seconds, so *which statistic over which runs* is the difference between a figure and a number. **If the basis cannot be reconstructed from what was recorded, say so — architect can write a bound and cannot write a false basis.** An unreconstructable basis is itself the finding and a better outcome than a plausible guess. Answer to architect directly.

**Two live sessions exist under the tauri-app-dev role, sharing `.worktrees/tauri-app` and its `target/`.** `TaskStop` did not terminate the first. **`tauri-app-dev-2` owns #105 and that worktree**; the original owns nothing further in it, has had #90 landed by cherry-pick, and should route #105-adjacent traffic to the other instance. This is the confirmed second cause of the LNK1104 failures the whole team had been treating as transient Windows file-locking — two cargo processes writing one `target/` (F-367's second cause, identified).

**Ratified on #105's mechanism (architect's two calls, both taken).** The **structural check on `boot.js` is dropped** — it is a source-shape guard, the family F-377 condemned, and since the built bundle is committed it would post-#98 be parsing Vite output: fragile at best and worthless if minified. What replaces it is the frontend evidence in `app/src/startup.ts`, which is #108 and is **already done** (vue-frontend-dev, `19fb1d0`, stacked on `task/98-mount-codemirror`) — it sends `raf1_ms`/`raf2_ms` with a paint anchor, delivered on the existing call rather than a second IPC round trip so it does not perturb the interval it measures. **Corrected at `9d76ba7`, and the correction is the lead's error rather than the author's:** this note first said the anchor was `nav_dom_content_loaded_ms` and that this *was* F-388's conjunction. **It is not.** That field is `PerformanceNavigationTiming`'s DOMContentLoaded — it fires when HTML parsing completes, **before any script runs, Vue's mount included** — so it is early regardless of when real content appears and cannot expose the mutation F-388 describes: two honestly-awaited frames scheduled before this app has painted anything. F-388 asks for **the paint entry**, and the field that answers it is **`first_contentful_paint_ms`**, read from the Paint Timing API's `first-contentful-paint` — the browser's own record of painting real content, which an empty `#app` div cannot produce before Vue mounts. **That is the field #105's guard checks `raf2_ms` against.** `nav_dom_content_loaded_ms` is kept and re-documented as **diagnostic-only**, since it still answers ADR-0014's separate `S_shell`-split question. Caught by vue-frontend-dev against the lead's ratification note. The **manual WebView2 mutation run is accepted for P0 as a stated bound, not as a passing test**, on two conditions architect set and I am keeping: it is recorded so nobody later reads *"mutations verified"* as meaning a test asserts it, and **it runs after #98 lands**. **A third condition is added on vue-frontend-dev's own flag: that run must also confirm the `first-contentful-paint` entry actually populates inside a real Tauri/WebView2 window.** Nobody has verified that it does — the smoke tests check exit code and trace completeness, not console-level values — and a guard built on an entry that never arrives would reject every honest mark instead of the dishonest one, which is F-107's shape (the guard fires and means the wrong thing), since a manual run against the `boot.js` about to be replaced validates a file on its way out — F-378's shape.

**Merge order is now constrained by a stack: `task/98-mount-codemirror` → `task/108-paint-evidence`.** #108's only artifact depends on files #98 adds, so #98 merges first or the two merge together.

### Lead rulings, third set — the built webview bundle, and what NFR-PRF-03 measures

**The built `webview/` output is committed, and that is a P0 decision with an explicit expiry.** #98's Vite build writes `crates/serat-app/webview/{index.html,boot.js,shell.css}` with fixed, non-hashed filenames. Nothing runs `npm run build` before `cargo test --workspace`, so a gitignored dist would break a fresh checkout on every machine that had not separately built the frontend — and with CI producing nothing (see below) there is no other place the gap could be covered. Committing build output is unlovely and it is the right trade here: the alternative is a workspace whose test suite depends on an unstated manual step. **vue-frontend-dev raised this rather than resolving it unilaterally, which was correct.** Two conditions travel with the ruling. The filenames stay non-hashed, because `the_placeholder_bundle_exists_and_carries_the_two_marks` opens them by literal name and hashing would spring **F-378** silently. And when CI runs again, a build-then-test step is the proper fix and this ruling is withdrawn.

**Open question for architect and test-engineer, and it decides what the gate's save-to-index number means.** `bench/budget/src/subject.rs:517`'s `save_to_index` calls `IndexHandle::apply()` and `flush()` **directly against serat-index**. It never enters `serat-app`, so it skips `AppState::write_file`, `write_atomic`, the re-read and hash, echo suppression, and the handle slot entirely. NFR-PRF-03's words are *"a note saved through **Serat's own write path**"*; §Open design questions says TC-021 *"times the real save path through `apply()` and `flush()`"*. **Those read differently and the difference is not cosmetic:** a harness that bypasses the slot cannot detect **F-382**'s degradation, because it never touches the thing that degrades. Settle it before #94 is built; the lead rules if the two disagree.

### Lead rulings, second set — #97's duplication, F-136's scope, and how finding numbers are issued

**#97 was built twice, and that is my failure rather than either author's.** test-engineer's spawn brief listed #97 among their tasks; my later ruling at `b116ba0` gave it to rust-core-dev to lead. I never withdrew it from the first list, so two people implemented T1 in parallel — test-engineer's `93b4d26` on `task/91-93-97-test-recovery`, and rust-core-dev's `c01698d` on `task/97-rebuild-equivalence`. Both are good work and one of them was avoidable.

**Ruling: `task/97-rebuild-equivalence` (`c01698d`) is T1.** It is real-vault-on-real-disk, which is the binding gap, and it snapshots `notes`, `blocks`, `fields`, `links`, `tasks` and a `blocks_fts` query through a natural key. test-engineer drops the #97 commit from their branch and keeps #91 and #93; **anything their version asserts that rust-core's does not is folded in rather than lost**, and code-reviewer names those, having read both.

**F-136's scope, narrowed — my ruling over-claimed and code-reviewer is right to have caught it.** I wrote that T1 is where F-136 closes. F-136's own text scopes itself to a 124 MB index and the 4 KB mid-file corruption case, and says it belongs in P1 with the read pool. **T1 closes the real-vault-on-real-disk half and no more: detection, reopen and row-level equivalence against a vault that actually exists on disk. The 124 MB and mid-file-corruption halves stay P1 and remain open.** A closure that does not quite reach is worse than an open caveat, and #101 must report it this way.

**How finding numbers are issued, because this has now cost three collisions in one wave.** Two different F-377s and two different F-378s exist on branches, plus the earlier double-#105 — every one from an author appending to a numbered list from a local view of a file that had moved. The authors did nothing wrong; **a number minted from a branch is not an identifier**, and the ID scheme's one permanent rule is that IDs never repeat.

So: **write the finding with `F-NEXT/<short-slug>` in place of a number and tell the lead. The lead issues the number at merge.** Numbers already issued in this wave: **F-383** to rust-core-dev (the abandoned-probe finding), **F-384** and **F-385** to test-engineer (`ci.yml`/`budgets.yml` cannot be scheduled; #91 closed by discard). Task #105's IDs column cites F-377 and means the marks guard, which is the copy on main.

**Carried, not adopted:** F-14, F-33 and F-34 are open against rust-core-dev from before the resume and are not on this wave's list — `write_atomic` widening permissions on a chmod'd file and the parent-directory fsync among them. They are not being pulled into P0 scope; #101 lists them as carried so the gate report does not read as though P0 left nothing open.

**Amendment to the #105/#106 ownership ruling — architect objected, the objection was right, and the fix is neither of the two proposals.** My ruling gave test-engineer every `trace.rs` change *in both tasks*, which put one person on one file across two concurrent branches — worse than the collision it was written to prevent, and F-07's cost in the shape I claimed to be avoiding. Architect proposed folding the harness work into #105 instead; that removes the two branches but makes one task multi-owner across two crates. **So: #105 is `crates/serat-app` only, and #106 carries every `trace.rs` change — the three missing fields, the sum-identity rejection, and F-377's post-composite rejection.** One file, one task, one owner, one branch. Architect stays on #105's mechanism with tauri-app-dev.

**F-377 is more urgent than its own row said, and architect is the one who found out why.** The mutation that survives the marks guard — `mark_first_paint` hoisted to script start — lands inside `S_shell` (`window_created` → `window_visible`), which ADR-0014 makes the **binding front-end term**. So an early mark does not flatter the total: **it empties the one term the re-baseline puts a number on.** The guard is not protecting a 400 ms budget that WebView2 misses regardless; it is protecting the split's only measurable claim about us. Recorded in §3.4 and ADR-0014 §Consequences.

**Sequencing ruling, and an ownership split, both following F-381.** The order is **#106 → #100 → #101 → #104**: the harness must be able to compute the Serat term before cold start is re-measured against it, and the requirement may not be re-baselined against a quantity nothing can measure. This is what puts a harness task on the critical path to the owner's own ruling.

**#105 and #106 both touch `bench/budget/src/trace.rs`, and they are deliberately not merged into one task.** `bench/` is test-engineer's by the ownership table, so **test-engineer owns every `trace.rs` change in both**, and tauri-app-dev owns #105's `crates/serat-app` half. Folding them into one sitting was proposed and is declined: three roles editing one file concurrently is RISK-002's exact shape (F-07 — the workspace is one shared object with many owners, and the cost scales with teammates rather than lines of code). They land together and are reviewed together; they are not written by the same hand.

**The two tasks may also do part of each other's work, which is worth checking rather than hoping.** Under F-377's mutation B the paint mark moves earlier without the elapsed time going anywhere, so a partition that must sum by construction should surface it as an implausible `S_shell` — the only term that moves if #98 takes the mark early. If #106's identity check catches it, #105 gets simpler; if it does not, tauri-app-dev needs to know that before designing the guard.

**Task numbers are the lead's to issue, and #105 is taken.** Two different #105s existed within the hour — the lead's marks guard (`3bf75b7`, on main) and the architect's trace-parsing task, written on a branch based on a commit that predated it. Neither author did anything wrong: both followed the standing instruction to create implementation tasks, from trees that disagreed. **This is F-376's class one level up — an identifier that two people can mint independently is not an identifier**, and the register it indexes is the one artifact the gate report is assembled from. So: propose the task, and the lead issues the number. Findings keep the same rule; the lead records those, and code-reviewer is read-only by role and must not be pushed into editing the register to get a finding written down.

**The measurement window rule, which is a scheduling constraint and not advice.** #83 attested its figures with `concurrent_build_processes: 0`, and F-327 records 35% session-to-session movement on unchanged code. **Tasks 95, 96 and 100 are measurements; no other teammate runs a cargo build while one is in progress.** The lead declares the window and holds it. This is RISK-002's real shape (F-07): the workspace is one shared object with many owners, and the cost scales with teammates rather than lines of code.

**Commit before your session ends.** Three worktrees were found carrying uncommitted work at resume - the same failure the last two sessions recorded. An uncommitted branch is indistinguishable from work never done.

## Out of scope for P0

No product features (charter). No live preview (P1, ADR-0006), no wikilink resolution UI, no SQ, no extraction, no AI, no packaging. Nothing on the charter's "deliberately not" list is touched.

## Findings so far (P0 in progress)

Recorded as they land, so the gate report is assembled from evidence rather than memory. Each names who measured it.

| # | Finding | Evidence | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| F-01 | **FTS5 is present in the SQLite Serat ships.** The DDL applies in full through rusqlite with no statement erroring, so architecture.md §4's "runs as delivered" is true of the bundled build (SQLite 3.53.2, `ENABLE_FTS5`). The earlier failure at line 292 came from the system `sqlite3` 3.42 on PATH — the Android platform-tools binary, built without fts5. A fact about that binary, not about Serat. Supersedes the caveat in commit d3b024a. | rust-core-dev, measured through rusqlite | Closed |
| F-02 | **The FTS5 external-content delete path holds.** Parent note deleted, block removed by `ON DELETE CASCADE`, blocks after cascade 0, FTS hits 0, and `INSERT INTO blocks_fts(blocks_fts) VALUES ('integrity-check')` returns Ok — internally consistent, not merely quiet. This is the case §4.4 singles out as breaking silently. | rust-core-dev | Closed |
| F-03 | **RESOLVED — there was never a conflict; the design document was missing a paragraph.** F-03 conflated two paths into the index. A save made *in Serat* goes write → parse pool → writer with **no watcher and no debounce**, and that is NFR-PRF-03's 50 ms. An *external* change goes through the 150 ms coalescing window and is FR-VLT-04, whose own acceptance criterion is 500 ms — it allows 500 precisely because it has a debounce to absorb. The two requirements never conflicted. What makes this a discovery rather than a rationalisation: **§3.2 already requires the save path.** Echo suppression *drops* the watcher event for Serat's own write, so an index fed only by the watcher would never index a note saved in Serat at all — the mechanism that exists to prevent duplicated work would prevent all of it. For product-analyst at Q1 this is a wording clarification naming the path and the interval, not a re-baseline of the number. The lead's earlier framing — "unsatisfiable as written" — was right about the watcher path and wrong to imply the requirement was unmeetable. | rust-core-dev found it; architect ruled | Closed |
| F-04 | **`rebuild()` has no legal home under §2.1.** It walks, parses and writes, and no product crate may do two of those; the budget harness and T1 must both run without Tauri. Lead's interim ruling: build `rebuild(source: &dyn NoteSource, parser: &dyn NoteParser, index_dir: &Path)` in serat-index — traits-in-core is the mechanism §2.1 prescribes and adds no dependency edge. The *home* (serat-index vs a new serat-pipeline crate) stays architect's, because the signature is expensive and the file move is not. | rust-core-dev; ruled by lead | Open — architect to ratify or relocate |
| F-05 | **ADR-0001's channel is not fire-and-forget.** `flush()` had to exist on day one: the rebuild runs ANALYZE after the last batch and every test needs read-after-write. The true shape is *one writer, asynchronous, with an explicit and expensive barrier*. Invisible in P0; surfaces in I5 when the UI reports "indexed" after a save, and in P2 when a write-back refreshes the row it changed. §2.2 describes a frictionless channel and should be amended. | rust-core-dev | Open — architect to amend §2.2 |
| F-06 | **Backpressure was undefined and had to be decided in code.** Review verdict: the *policy* is defensible and needs no ADR to permit it; what an ADR should record is **which producer may be dropped**, not whether dropping is allowed. See F-11 for the implementation defect. A full bounded queue drops the path, latches a rescan flag, and lets the consumer walk and reconcile. Blocking the producer instead blocks the watcher's coalescer, overflowing the OS event buffer — losing events with no record that anything was lost, the one failure C-02 cannot absorb. "Bounded" in §3.1 step 2 reads as a memory statement when it is a correctness one. | rust-core-dev, forced by I2 | Open — ADR-0011 candidate |
| F-07 | **A-06 / RISK-002: the Rust is not the risk.** serat-vault compiled first try, 35 of 40 tests passed on the first run, two clippy findings across two tasks, rusqlite bundled built SQLite unattended. Nothing about the language cost an hour. All three real costs were coordination: a workspace-wide gate red because another teammate's crate was mid-edit; the repository appearing mid-task; and untracked `bench/` breaking `cargo metadata` in every worktree at once. The risk is that the workspace is one shared object with many owners, and that cost scales with teammates rather than lines of code. | rust-core-dev | Open — informs the RISK-002 re-rating at gate |
| F-08 | **`PRAGMA foreign_keys` is ON by default in our build** (`DEFAULT_FOREIGN_KEYS` is compiled in), so cascade fires without setting it. Set it explicitly anyway — depending on a vendored build flag is what changes silently at a version bump — but the reason is defensiveness, not necessity, and the stated reason matters to whoever debugs it next. | rust-core-dev, proved by running the cascade without the pragma | Closed |
| F-09 | **§1.2's three assumptions: P0 has produced evidence on none of them.** (a) *Write contention is the bottleneck* — not tested; first measurable in I4. What writing the types showed is structural, not empirical: the shape is sound in that no message needs a reply, and weaker than §2.2 claims in that `flush()` is a mandatory serialising barrier. Record as neither confirmed nor contradicted. (b) *Parsing dominates rebuild time* — not tested; this is open design question 1 and needs I4, I5 and B2 against the 20k vault. (c) *Brute-force vector search at 200,000 chunks* — untouched, a P4 question, and ADR-0009 was written to be reversed cheaply. The A-06 coordination finding (F-07) is real but is a finding about how the team works, not about the three structures §1.2 puts at risk. A status document implying otherwise overstates the spike. | rust-core-dev | Open — settled by T2 |
| F-10 | **A single rebuild number cannot answer open design question 1.** The rebuild's cost splits four ways — walk, read+hash, parse, write — because the walk enumerates without hashing. Reported as one number, "the index writer dominates" and "reading 39.4 MiB off disk dominates" are indistinguishable, and they imply completely different fixes. B2 must report the four phases separately before it hardens; otherwise the P0 gate produces a number that passes or fails without saying what to change. | rust-core-dev, from I2's walk | **Implemented in B2** (`2186da0`). `prf04-full-rebuild` records walk / read+hash / parse / write beside the aggregate, in ms and as a share of wall clock, in both report and result JSON; the budget still asserts the total. Two decisions worth keeping: the phases are the **per-phase median across runs**, not the phases of the median run, so one slow run cannot decide where the time went; and the phases may sum to **less** than the wall clock, which the report states explicitly, because that is concurrency between the parse pool and the writer — a sum *exceeding* the total would be the anomaly. The job also fails a rebuild whose reported note count disagrees with the vault manifest, since a fast number over a partially indexed vault is not a passing budget and a wall clock cannot see that. |
| F-11 | **The drop-latch-rescan recovery path re-enters the loss, and it loses files permanently.** Queue capacity is 8,192; the P0 vault is 20,200 files; `walk()` returns sorted paths and the queue is FIFO, so every rescan pushes the same sequence and the same 8,192 paths win the slots. Running the documented contract exactly — full walk, push all, drain, check latch — **12,008 of 20,200 files had never once reached the consumer after five rescans.** A stable loop, not a slow one. It also means the *first* index of a 20k vault drops ~12k paths unless the consumer outpaces the walk, which a cold start cannot promise. Root cause: the bulk producer is routed through the bounded queue that drops. Fix: the walk hands `Vec<VaultEntry>` to the consumer directly, or `push` gets two modes so the walk blocks while the watcher drops — blocking the walk is safe because it is not the coalescer, so the OS-event-buffer argument does not apply to it. | code-reviewer, reproduced | Open — BLOCKING on I2 |
| F-12 | **`Vault::walk` costs ~2.0 s on the 20k vault, against an NFR-PRF-01 budget of 1 s total.** It calls `fs::canonicalize` per entry; the same traversal with the prefix stripped lexically takes ~50 ms — a 40x difference, spent before a single byte is parsed. Whether this is fatal or merely wasteful depends on what D2 decides the warm-start path actually does, which is one more reason D2 is the critical path. | code-reviewer, measured | **Answered by D2, still a defect.** §3.4 puts the walk off the cold-start critical path: with a warm index the tree renders from one query over `notes(path)` (~10 ms) and the walk runs afterwards as catch-up, so NFR-PRF-01 does not depend on walk speed. It remains worth fixing on two grounds — with no index (first open, or after a rebuild) the tree *does* come from the streaming walk, and that path carries FR-VLT-01's 2 s budget, which a 2.0 s walk fails on its own; and canonicalise-per-entry is pure waste. Open — rust-core-dev to strip the prefix lexically. |
| F-13 | **Three guards on I2 cannot fail, and one mechanism is not wired at all.** (a) Both atomic-write tests pass unchanged against a plain truncate-in-place `fs::write` — the "leaves no temp files behind" test especially, since an implementation with no temp files trivially leaves none. FR-VLT-03's kill-mid-write criterion is unasserted. (b) The coalescing test asserts the queue's path dedup, not the 150 ms window: with `COALESCE_WINDOW` set to 0 it passes identically. (c) Echo suppression matches §3.2 and prunes correctly, but **nothing calls it** — `write_atomic` never records the hash it is about to produce, so §3.2 is not implemented end to end. `RescanRequired` has zero coverage, which is why F-11's sibling defect went unnoticed. | code-reviewer, each verified by mutation | Open — BLOCKING/SHOULD-FIX on I2 |
| F-14 | **Threat T-06 has no guard.** `write_atomic` to a `.pdf` returns `Ok`, and the crate's own asset fixture does exactly that. Cheap to close now; much harder once serat-ai and serat-mcp reach for that API. | code-reviewer | Open — rust-core-dev |
| F-15 | **A folder rename never reaches the index.** Event handling never checks whether a path is a directory: renaming a folder holding two notes put the two *directory* paths on the queue as work items, raised no rescan, and none of the four note paths — old or new — reached anything. The index keeps rows for files that no longer exist and never learns the new ones. `git mv` and any vault reorganisation hit this. | code-reviewer, reproduced | Open — BLOCKING on I2 |
| F-16 | **The PRD's data dictionary contradicts the DDL, and the DDL is the correct copy.** The appendix names the FTS table `notes_fts` and omits `val_bool` and `fields.id`; `schema/001_init.sql` has `blocks_fts`, `val_bool` and `fields.id`. A note-level FTS table cannot answer *which block*, so it cannot serve a citation or a locator (FR-AIX, FR-BIN), and `fields.id` is the `__fid` every editable cell carries under ADR-0008. Nobody should treat the dictionary as the spec in the meantime. | architect, building D1 | Open — product-analyst to reconcile the appendix at Q1 |
| F-17 | **`ix_notes_type` is now partial on `type IS NOT NULL`.** On a 20k fixture with 500 typed notes, ANALYZE reports "500 500" rather than "20000 10000" and the index holds 500 entries instead of 20,000; the query plan is unchanged. Accepted cost: `GROUP BY type` becomes a scan plus a temp b-tree. Recorded because §4.4 previously described the non-partial form. | architect, measured | Closed — DDL and §4.4 agree |
| F-18 | **The three `ix_fields_key_*` partial indexes need no redundant `IS NOT NULL` from the lowering.** `=`, `<`, `BETWEEN`, `IN` and LIKE-prefix all select the partial index unaided; only a bare `key = ?` with no predicate on the value cannot use them. §4.4 assumed the workaround would be required, and P2 should not build it. | architect, measured | Closed — recorded in §4.4 before P2 |
| F-19 | **The rebuild dropped files under backpressure, and would have posted a *fast* benchmark number for a partly indexed vault.** `submit` refuses when the queue is full — correct on the watcher path, where a rescan rediscovers a dropped path; catastrophic in a rebuild, where nothing will ever mention that file again. This is F-11's defect in a second place, and the same root cause: one queue policy serving two producers with opposite recovery properties. Fixed with `submit_blocking`, deliberately **not** on the trait so the save path cannot reach for it, plus a 1,000-note test that outruns the 512-op batch. `RebuildReport` now counts rows in the database after the writer drains, never operations sent — a report counting submissions cannot notice the writer losing them. Caught from two sides independently: this test, and test-engineer's harness check that a rebuild's note count must match the vault manifest. | rust-core-dev, in I4 | Closed |
| F-20 | **`blocks.kind` has no CHECK value for raw HTML, so HTML blocks are dropped rather than mislabelled.** Dropping costs their full-text hits, which design goal 5 says should degrade rather than disappear; mislabelling them as paragraphs would make `blocks.kind` mean "roughly what this is", which is the more expensive lie. Recorded at the mapping site with the reasoning. Costs nothing until I3 emits HTML blocks. | rust-core-dev, in I4 | **Closed — `blocks.kind` gains `'html'`** (and deliberately not `'rule'`). Verified by running. |
| F-21 | **`shutdown()` deadlocked, and the cause generalises.** It closed the channel by dropping its sender, but handles are clones, so "channel closed" means "the last clone went" — and the rebuild holds one for its whole run. `Stop` is now an explicit message. It hung a 600 s test run before it was found. Worth keeping in mind wherever a channel's lifetime is used as a signal. | rust-core-dev, in I4 | Closed |
| F-22 | **`links.kind` admitted `'tag'` while the comment beneath it claimed `dst_note IS NULL` was the authoritative test for an unresolved link.** A future re-resolution pass trusting that comment would have tried to resolve every tag in the vault, forever, finding nothing. Fixed structurally rather than by correcting the comment: the predicate is now `dst_note IS NULL AND kind <> 'tag'`, and a CHECK makes a row that would break it impossible to create. Found and fixed by its own author while answering someone else's questions. | architect, self-caught | Closed |
| F-23 | **ADR-0005 pins the sidecar filename to `<binary>.md`** (`lease.pdf` → `lease.pdf.md`). Replacing the extension collides: `lease.pdf` and `lease.png` would share one sidecar, and a vault already containing a genuine note named `lease.md` would have it silently adopted as an asset's metadata. test-engineer's 200 generated assets are correct as they stand and need no regeneration. | architect | Closed |
| F-24 | **The cold-start path does no per-file work at all** — which is the design's real answer to RISK-012. Not "per-file work is fast on a machine with the scanner disabled", but that there is none on the critical path. §3.4 also records that nothing in the path assumes a git repository (whether the vault is one is the user's business), and that the open check deliberately does not touch `blocks_fts`, because an FTS integrity check costs 390 ms and a stale search index should degrade rather than trigger a full rebuild. | architect, in D2/§3.4 | Closed |
| F-25 | **The budget harness's statistic is only half pinned, and the unpinned half flatters the number.** Nearest-rank p95 is correct at n=20 and n=1000, and the n=20 test bites if the rank goes off by one upward - but changing `.ceil()` to `.floor()` leaves every test green, because at n=20 and n=1000 the two coincide. At **n=3 they do not**: `floor(0.5 x 3) = 1` yields `sorted[0]`, the *fastest* of three, where `ceil` yields the middle. One character silently turns NFR-PRF-04's median-of-three into a best-of-three. Likewise `WallClock => percentile(0.50)` can become `percentile(0.01)` with everything still green: nothing asserts the median at all. The report also prints `measured wall clock = X ms` over n=3 without saying the figure is a median - and that line goes into the gate report. | code-reviewer, by mutation | Open - B4, before any gate number is taken |
| F-26 | **The vault digest gate proves self-consistency, not canonicality - a *wrong* vault passes.** Regenerating the 20k vault with `--seed 424242` gives digest `da0d24b7...` instead of the canonical `35fcb867...`, and `prf04-full-rebuild` went straight through the gate to the seam. The manifest is regenerated alongside the vault, so the two always agree. `vaultgen` itself prints "seed differs from the canonical ... not comparable with recorded ones" and the harness never sees it. The canonical digest is pinned in `budgets.yml` - in the `vault` job, on a different runner, and since `needs: vault` shares no artifact, the jobs that actually produce numbers never check it. Three lines in `ensure_vault` close it. | code-reviewer, reproduced | Open - B4, before any gate number is taken |
| F-27 | **Four mutations of the budget harness are invisible to all nine of its tests**: a job swallowing the seam error and posting a fabricated 1 ms number; removing `run_job`'s "no samples is a failure" branch; deleting the note-count guard that F-19 exists to enforce; and never printing or recording the phase breakdown. The three job binaries have no coverage at all, and `run_job` is untestable as written because it returns an opaque `ExitCode`. Separately the `every-budget-has-a-job` meta-job **passes when its grep matches nothing**: reformat `id: "x"` to `id:"x"` and the id count goes 4 to 0, the loop body never runs, and the job exits 0 having reviewed nothing. The job whose purpose is catching an unasserted budget is itself unasserted. | code-reviewer, by mutation | Open - B4 |
| F-28 | **The worktree guard still cannot fail - third instance of the class, and this one is on main.** With a full workspace copy inside `.worktrees/rust-core` and `exclude = [".worktrees"]` deleted from the root manifest, `the_check_ignores_teammate_worktrees` passes, along with the other 13 tests: `members` is an explicit list with no globs, so `cargo metadata --no-deps` can never report a manifest under `.worktrees/` whether the exclude is present or not. The earlier fix stopped a test failing for the wrong reason; it did not produce a test that can fail for the right one. Both I2 and B2 carry the same version. Assert the property against the manifest text, where it can actually break, and delete the comment claiming a tree-walking checker would find six copies. | code-reviewer, by mutation | Open - rust-core-dev |
| F-29 | **`LoadedNote` (core) duplicates `LoadedFile` (serat-vault) field for field**, doc comment nearly word for word. When I2 merges there will be two identical types and `Vault::read` will return the wrong one for `NoteSource::load`. Deleting `LoadedFile` in favour of `serat_core::LoadedNote` is a condition on the I2 revision - a domain type with no I/O belongs in core, cheap now and a pervasive rename later. Related: **`NoteSource::entries()` returns `Vec<VaultPath>` and discards the walk's kind, size and mtime**, which `Vault::walk()` has already collected, so a rebuild re-derives kind from the extension and re-stats - 20,000 stats collected and thrown away, against a 30 s budget in which the walk alone already costs 2 s. Returning the richer entry costs nothing while the trait is provisional. | code-reviewer | Open - rust-core-dev, architect to confirm the trait shape |
| F-30 | **Provenance note, recorded here because the commit message does not carry it.** Commit `8c5730f` on main is described as the worktree-guard fix, but it also introduced `NoteSource` and `LoadedNote` into serat-core (~93 lines). The lead cherry-picked it to unblock three teammates red on a guard that could not fail, and did not narrow the commit first. `git log` therefore does not show where those two types entered the codebase; this row is where. Reviewed after the fact and found additive, declaring no I/O, with `serat_core_performs_no_io` still holding. | lead; reviewed by code-reviewer | Closed |
| F-31 | **ADR-0011's "clear on completion" is still wrong, one level in — a defect in the ADR, not in the implementation.** An overflow occurring *during* the rescan is cleared by that rescan finishing, so the walk meant to recover the earlier drops silently swallows a newer set: the same failure the ADR exists to prevent. The latch needs a generation counter — read it when the walk starts, clear only if it has not moved, walk again if it has. Found by its own author reading I2's `queue.rs` against the ADR, the first time that decision met an implementation. Recorded against ADR-0011 rather than against I2, which is where the under-specification was. Note for the reviewer: `queue.rs` has a test asserting the second `take_overflowed` returns false, so it currently pins the wrong invariant and a correct fix will read as a regression to anyone diffing without the ADR. | architect, self-caught | Open - rust-core-dev implements; property fixed, signature theirs |
| F-32 | **Leaving the FTS triggers live during a bulk load costs about 6x on the phase that must fit inside a 30 s budget.** Sound measurement: **26,000 blocks at 2.1 s with triggers live against 0.32 s dropped**, taken after the harness was fixed, plus the transaction-count table; independently reproduced by code-reviewer at **5.8x** (1.57 s against 0.272 s), with an identical resulting search index. This is why the rebuild drops and re-executes triggers read back from `sqlite_master`, and why `write` splits into write-rows and write-FTS. **STRUCK — the previously recorded "260,000 blocks unfinished after 25 minutes with triggers live" is not a sound measurement and must not be quoted.** Its author observed it, then withdrew it: that run inserted row by row through Python rather than in batches, under a transaction regime later found to be confused (an explicit BEGIN issued against a connection managing its own transactions). It is an abandoned run, not a measurement. The lead repeated it as fact before it was withdrawn. Real behaviour at 260k blocks is T2's to measure on the real pipeline. | architect, measured then self-corrected; reproduced by code-reviewer | Closed at 6x; the 25-minute figure struck |
| F-33 | **`write_atomic` uses `File::create` default permissions, so replacing a note the user chmod'd 600 silently widens it.** Design goal 1 is that the vault survives the application; an application that quietly relaxes the permissions on a user's private file fails that. Distinct from the acceptance criterion, which is only that the file is wholly old or wholly new. | rust-core-dev, self-reported | Open - rust-core-dev |
| F-34 | **`write_atomic` does not `sync_all` the parent directory**, so on POSIX the rename may not survive a power cut. FR-VLT-03's stated criterion - wholly old or wholly new - still holds, so this is not a breach; it is the gap between that criterion and what a user would assume it means. | rust-core-dev, self-reported | Open - rust-core-dev, or P1 with FR-VLT-03 |
| F-35 | **A fifth test that could not fail, self-reported.** `four_thousand_files_become_four_thousand_entries_not_more` used 4,000 files against a queue capacity of 8,192, so no test ever crossed the boundary it existed to probe - a number chosen to make the test pass rather than to make it bite. Recorded because the pattern, not the instance, is the finding: five occurrences across three teammates in one spike, every one found by mutation or by boundary arithmetic and none by reading. | rust-core-dev, self-reported | Open - sweep of serat-index before the I4 review |
| F-36 | **The blank-rectangle trap is now a hard test rather than an instruction.** `bench/budget/src/trace.rs` refuses a cold-start trace four ways: `t0_source` must be `harness` (a figure taken inside `main` undercounts pre-main and is a different measurement, not a worse one); `profile` must be `release`; marks must be monotonic (a sample that survived a clock step is not a sample); and **`window_visible - window_created >= 5 ms`**, since compositing a first frame costs far more and a paint mark rewired to window creation collapses the gap to ~0. Complementary shell-side property: no Rust path stamps the paint mark, so a shell painting nothing never marks, times out, and exits non-zero. | test-engineer and tauri-app-dev, agreed between themselves | Closed |
| F-37 | **§3.4's load-bearing claim is now an assertion.** `prf01-cold-start` runs against the 20k **and** 50k vaults and fails if window-painted p95 grows more than 20%. A path proportional to vault size would show ~2.5x, so the threshold sits far below what it catches and comfortably above noise. Open question routed to architect: is "nothing on the critical path is proportional to vault size" meant strictly? An assertion stronger than the design promises will eventually fail for the wrong reason and be deleted by someone in a hurry. | test-engineer | **Answered, and the question exposed a missing distinction.** `t_visible` is strictly constant — before the first paint the main thread reads a small settings file, creates a window, loads a bundle compiled into the binary, and paints with `IndexState::Opening`. The 20% assertion is sound there and a violation is a real defect. **`t_usable` is not constant** and asserting that it is would eventually fail legitimately: the file tree is one query returning every path, so it grows linearly in note count, and watcher registration grows in the number of *directories* on Linux, where inotify takes one watch per directory against a single call on Windows and macOS. `t_usable` is bounded and sub-linear, held by NFR-PRF-01's absolute budget with the 50k figure recorded beside it, not by a growth ratio. §3.4 now states both, because extending the growth assertion from one mark to the other is the obvious next move for whoever touches that job and would look like tightening the gate while introducing a platform-dependent flake. 20% stays: it catches a ~2.5x, so it has a factor of twelve of headroom, and a flaky budget job gets disabled by someone in a hurry. |
| F-38 | **The five-phase breakdown needs `write-FTS` asserted non-zero, or it can flatter a regression.** write-rows and write-FTS are only comparable across runs if both are measured on the bulk-load shape. If a rebuild ever regressed to loading *through* the triggers, write-FTS would read as roughly zero and write-rows would absorb the whole cost — **the breakdown would look better while the total got worse**, and the failure is invisible in a total. Asserting write-FTS is non-zero catches it cheaply. Given F-32 (about 6x at 26,000 blocks, reproduced independently at 5.8x), this is the regression most worth catching in the rebuild path. | architect, unprompted | Open - B4 or T2 |

Recorded because the general rule is worth more than the instance: **the design file is authoritative about the mechanism, not about its author's preferences among equivalent names** — "where this document and those files disagree, the files are correct" cuts both ways. | F-40 | **A guard that cannot be deleted beats a guard that merely passes — and the difference has been luck rather than design.** The reviewer deleted test-engineer's note-count guard and all nine of the harness's tests still passed: one of the two mechanisms protecting against F-19 could be removed with nothing going red. Its author's own assessment: "that's luck as much as design — I chose it to keep the trait narrow, not because I'd reasoned about deletability." The question to ask deliberately from here is **which of these guards can be removed without a test going red**, which is the question mutation testing answers, and the stronger move is a structural guarantee that no deletion can reach. | F-43 | **Truncation detection is scale-dependent, and §3.4 states it as a property of truncation.** At a 19.4 MB index, truncating by 0.1%, 1%, 50% and to 100 bytes all failed instantly at `PRAGMA user_version`, as documented. At a **2.0 MB index, truncating by 0.1% passed all six steps of the stamp** — 2,114 bytes is under one page, and nothing the stamp reads touches the tail. The original measurement used a 124 MB fixture, so the conclusion holds at the scale that matters and the choice of the stamp is unaffected. But "the file was truncated ... and every statement failed instantly" reads as general, and a reader taking it that way would conclude the lazy `IndexFault` path is optional. One clause fixes it: name the fixture size, or say the smallest truncation tested removed more than a page. | code-reviewer, reproduced at two scales; **diagnosis corrected by architect at a third** | **Closed, and the rule is not scale — it is one page.** SQLite derives the page count from the file length by integer division, so a cut of **less than a whole page** leaves that count unchanged and is invisible. On the *same* 124 MB fixture the original numbers came from: 100 bytes passes, 2,048 bytes passes, **4,096 bytes (one page) is detected**, 0.1% (30 pages) detected. The identical boundary holds on a 1.4 MB database. A percentage misled only because 0.1% of a large index is thirty pages and of a small one is half a page — which is exactly why the 2.0 MB case passed and the 124 MB case failed. **The `quick_check` claim is WITHDRAWN — the honest answer is undetermined.** Re-run with the rows printed rather than the comparison trusted: on the 124 MB fixture `quick_check` returns exactly one `'ok'` row for both sub-page cuts, while on 1.2 MB and 15.6 MB databases the *same* 2,048-byte cut is reported as real corruption. Both results are real; the difference is **what the removed bytes held** — free space at a file's tail goes unnoticed, live cell data does not. Neither result generalises, so neither belongs in an argument, and "detected by nothing at open, not even the expensive option" is struck. The `IndexFault` decision never needed it: it rests on the 4 KB mid-file overwrite, which both parties reproduced, plus the 1,150 ms cost. **The one-page threshold for the stamp is unaffected and now cites four databases across two orders of magnitude** — 1.2, 1.4, 15.6 and 124 MB, both parties' fixtures together. |

| F-45 | **The rebuild self-heals across a crash only because one line makes it so.** If the process dies between dropping the triggers and restoring them, `index_meta.state` is still `'building'`, so the next open rebuilds — and that holds *only* because step 6 is the sole place `state` becomes `'ready'`. | F-46 | **S1 sets `t_usable` unconditionally, diverging from §3.4's definition.** §3.4 defines `t_usable` as all three of the open check finishing, the file tree being delivered, and **the watcher being registered**; `mark_vault_usable` consults nothing about the vault thread's stage. Fix directed: gate on the watcher stage having **resolved** rather than on `WatcherRegistered`, since `WatcherFailed` is legitimate under design goal 5 (a vault with no watcher is stale, not broken), and record which outcome it was, because a usable number taken with no watcher is not comparable to one taken with one. | F-60 | **Correction to this register: the F-04 §2.1 text was never lost.** It was committed at `574f530` on `task/F04-rebuild-orchestration-home`, branched from main **in parallel** rather than stacked — item 4 of a merge-order list whose items 5-8 did stack. Corrected wording: the line is not "no files", it is **whose files**. serat-index owns `.serat/` and may create and delete the index directory; it may not open, walk, stat or read anything under the vault root. | F-62 | **The walk-defer and F-12 pair must be verified on the no-index path, not the warm one.** The warm path *cannot* regress from deferring the walk, because its tree comes from the index — so a benchmark measuring only warm start would show the improvement and hide the risk. The no-index path (first open, or after a rebuild) is the only path where the ordering change can hurt, and it is the one carrying FR-VLT-01's own 2 s budget. | architect, refining F-59 | Open - test-engineer, when the pair lands |
| F-63 | **Seventh instance, found on the approving pass and deliberately not made a condition of it.** Changing `pending.entry(path).or_insert(deadline)` to `pending.insert(...)` in `watch.rs` — extending the coalescing deadline on every event rather than leaving it fixed — **passes all 129 tests**. | F-64 | **When the property is about a rate, the setup must be asserted, not only the outcome.** The mirror guard that closed F-50 asserts the producer is *still waiting after 50 ms* before claiming anything about the outcome — which is what `the_watcher_still_drops_rather_than_blocking` had from the start and what the block-side lacked. Recorded because the gate report must not let a count of findings stand in for a judgement about quality. | code-reviewer, unprompted | Standing - carry into the gate report |
| F-66 | **The four rebuild phases carry two different labelling problems, and a reader who adds them up will be wrong twice.** `write` is **producer wait**, not the writer's total work (F-56). Both are correct measurements of the right things; neither is a share of the wall clock. **The gate report must label each phase with what it measures**, or the one number that settles a two-year-old design question gets read as arithmetic that does not add up. | rust-core-dev, flagging it before the numbers exist | Open - must be in the gate report and the harness output |
| F-67 | **An unparseable note is currently skipped rather than indexed as a bare row, and that is a design decision nobody has made.** Skipping means the vault's *contents* depend on whether the parser understood a file, which sits badly with design goal 5 (capability degrades, nothing breaks) and with NFR-REL-03. Its implementer briefly changed it, then reverted: a timing task is not the place to change what the index contains. Flagged at the function and left for a ruling. | rust-core-dev, self-reverted | **Ruled: bare row, never skipped.** Design goal 5 applied to Markdown instead of binaries — §7.3 had already settled the identical question, where a binary with no extractor "still indexes with its sidecar and stays typed, linkable and queryable", and **there is no reason a Markdown file should be treated worse than a PDF nobody can read**. Skipping is the option that looks safe and is not: the file exists, so a skipped note is missing from the tree and from path search, links to it read as unresolved, and no surface says why — the vault would contain a file the product denies. The bare row is path, hash, mtime, size and a filename title, with no blocks, fields or links. **No column records the failure**: it is re-derivable in the time it takes to read the file, so P1's validation surface can compute it, and a column would be a schema change — a rebuild under ADR-0002 — bought for a consumer that does not exist. Narrower in practice than it sounds: CommonMark has no parse failures and broken frontmatter already indexes the note and records the problem, so the real case is a `.md` file that is not valid UTF-8. |
